CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-20-24 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2024, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and Simon 
Contractors, hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the "Public Works Improvements" project all in strict accordance 
with the Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, numbered and dated: Addendum One 
(1), dated April 24, 2024, Addendum Two (2), dated April 26, 2024, and Addendum Three 
(3), dated April 29, 2024. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by October 31, 2025.  If the work has not been 
completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City 
for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated 
damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the 
work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, the sum of Two 
Million, Three Hundred Seventy-Seven Thousand, Seventeen Dollars, and Twenty-Five 
Cents ($2,377,017.25). In the event there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid 
Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract 
amount will be adjusted accordingly.  The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual 
services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for the percentage of 
estimated work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the 
payable due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or 
11:17 am, May 07 2024

Page 2 of 2 
the AIA Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval 
prior to payment. 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has 
authorized the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the 
parties hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written. 
 
ATTEST: 
 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING

Patrick Collins, Mayor 
 
Contractor 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Simon Contractors

Forty-Five Cents ($1,693,498.45). In the event there are changes in the estimated quantities 
shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the 
total contract amount will be adjusted accordingly.  The City agrees to pay the above amount for 
contractual services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for the percentage of 
estimated work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the 
payable due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request 
5:04 pm, May 07 2024

Page 2 of 2 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or 
the AIA Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval 
prior to payment. 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has 
authorized the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the 
parties hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written. 
 
ATTEST:

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-05-14_TotalExtraction_14513229.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282  
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director 
Subject: Submission of 2023 Urban Renewal Authority Annual Report 
Date: 
May 13, 2024   
Background: 
The attached report provides a summary of the Urban Renewal Authority’s activities over the prior 
calendar year. The annual report details assets, liabilities, income, and operating expenses for 2023 
and is required by Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-136 and Cheyenne Municipal Code § 2.80.050. 
This item will not be referred to committee. 
Attachments: 
1.
2023 Urban Renewal Authority Annual Report
Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

Page  1 of 3 
 
Urban Renewal 
Authority 
 
2023  
Annual Report 

Page  2 of 3 
 
The purpose of this report is to provide a summary of the Urban Renewal Authority’s activities over the 
prior calendar year.  The annual report details assets, liabilities, income, and operating expenses for 
2023.  
 
The purpose of this report is to comply with Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-136 and Cheyenne Municipal  
Code § 2.80.050, Annual Reports.   
Purpose 
Background 
Urban renewal is a state-enabled economic development tool used to revitalize deteriorating or 
underdeveloped areas. Redevelopment of urban areas is often more expensive than new development, 
so public action through an Urban Renewal Authority can be used to encourage private investment in 
the area. The Wyoming Urban Renewal Code enables municipalities to set up Urban Renewal Authorities 
which have the ability to designate Urban Renewal Projects. These projects can use a variety of tools for 
redevelopment, including tax increment financing (TIF).   
 
The Urban Renewal Authority (URA) is comprised of a board of five members knowledgeable in 
community planning, urban renewal, and business management. The URA reviews Urban Renewal Plans 
and Projects for specific areas and makes recommendations regarding the use of Tax Increment 
Financing (TIF). The Urban Renewal Plans and Projects and TIF-related development agreements require 
approval by the Governing Body.  
 
The URA was formed on June 28, 2021, with the passage of Ordinance No. 4381.  On June 28, 2021, 
the Governing Body adopted Resolution No. 6156 determining that blighted areas in the community 
allowing the URA to execute powers under the State of Wyoming Urban Renewal Code to authorize  
rehabilitation and redevelopment of these defined areas as necessary for the public safety, health, 
morals, and welfare for the residents of the City of Cheyenne.  
 
 
Urban Renewal 
Authority 
 
2023  
Annual Report 

Page  3 of 3 
 
 
Two active pledges exist for TIF projects 
regarding payments to the Hitching Post 
Plan and Project for environmental 
remediation and associated overages.  
 
Income / Balances 
 
Fiduciary account balances for the three 
Plan and Project areas (Increment plus 
interest) for active Plans and Projects are: 
1. Hitching Post: $4,407.09 
2. 15th Street: $18,300.87 
3. Hynds and the Hole: $1,195.12 
4. West Downtown Plan and Project: $0.00 
 
Disbursement of the of the 2023 tax 
increment is expected in May of 2024 
contingent on property tax payment by 
landowners within the Plan and Project area. 
 
Operating Expenses 
 
The URA is acting under the umbrella of the 
City in the Planning and Development 
Department.  The City is absorbing all costs 
associated with the URA at this time.  
 
Financial Statement 
 
Revenue: $0 
Expenses: $0 
Savings/Checking accounts: Not established 
Plan and Projects 
 
Active plans and projects include the:  
• 
Hitching Post Plan and Project   
Approved August 23, 2021 
• 
Hynds and Hole Plan and Project  
 
Approved June 13, 2022  
• 
15th Street Plan and Project  
 
Approved June 13, 2022  
• 
West Downtown Plan and Project 
 
Approved September 11, 2023 
 
The West Downtown Plan and Project was approved 
during calendar year of 2023. This plan and project 
encompasses six blocks in the downtown.  
 
Public Outreach 
 
Creation of Urban Renewal Authority Website, 
www.CheyenneCity.org/URA 
The Urban Renewal Authority had a joint City Council 
work session with the Downtown Development 
Authority on July 7th, 2023 to discuss the programs 
and answer any City Council questions.    
 
Assets 
 
The URA obtained no assets obtained in calendar year 
2023 other than tax increment revenue identified in 
the “Income / Balances” section of this report.   
 
Liabilities 
 
Through the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, 
the URA entered into a TIF development agreement 
pledging up to $460,000.00 of tax increment 
revenue collected for overages with the initial 
development agreement with Swagger Construction, 
LLC’s demolition and remediation of the Hitching 
Post.  In 2021 the URA entered into a TIF 
development agreement pledging up to $1.2 million 
of tax increment revenue collected for remediation of 
environmental hazards associated with Swagger  
Construction, LLC’s demolition and remediation of the 
Hitching Post. The development agreements limit 
reimbursement to collected tax increment funds or the 
25-year time period of the Hitching Post Urban 
Renewal Plan and Project and extends no financial 
commitment to the City or URA. 
 
Summary of Activities

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-05-08_TotalExtraction_37779309.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
May 13, 2024  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave (via Zoom) and 
Jeff White. Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda).  Upon request by Dr. Aldrich, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Johnson and Mr. Cook, item #23 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request 
by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. White, item #32(b) 
was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Esquibel moved to approve remaining items, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 22, 2024.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis to CB 
Community Business for land located at Lot 2, Block 1, Q-B Subdivision. Reporting for Public 
Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Esquibel.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4560)       
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, High 
Plains Road beginning at South Greeley Highway and terminating at Division Avenue.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4651) 
       
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of Saratoga Street and west of Whitney Road.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. Public comment was made by: Cotton Jones, Jones Land Surveying.  Comment 
was made by the following member of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich.  (#4562) 
 
 
 
 

 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of AR Agricultural Residential for land annexed 
to the City of Cheyenne located east of Saratoga Street and south of and adjacent to Laramie Street.  
Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4563) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located at 4204 Dell Range Boulevard.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
(#4564) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of City MR Medium Density Residential for land 
annexed to the City of Cheyenne located at 4204 Dell Range Boulevard. Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#4565)       
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to P Public District 
for land located at Lot 1, Cheyenne Ice & Events Center, 2nd Filing located at 1530 West 
Lincolnway.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4566) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Sections 1.4.3 Defined Terms and 5.8.8 
Accessory Buildings of the Unified Development Code, clarifying Detached Buildings.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”.  Public 
comment was made by: Victoria Pike.  Comments were made by the following members of the 
governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Ordinance No. 4519 amending the Official 
Zoning Map of the City of Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from HI Heavy Industrial, 
AG Agricultural, and County A-2 to LI Light Industrial for land located west of Burlington Trail 
and south of and adjacent to HR Ranch Road.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Subsections 6.5.4.b.3 Incentives / Bonus, 
6.5.4.b.4 Creative Sign Program, and 6.5.8.j Billboards of the Unified Development Code, to 
restructure sign incentives and bonuses by modifying allowances for signs near interstate 
highways, allowances for shared signs, eliminating the Creative Sign Program, and clarifying 
applicability of certain billboard allowances.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Mr. 
Johnson. 
 
 

 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 5.1.5 of the Unified Development 
Code to allow for up to three stories for detached dwellings, semi-attached dwellings, and attached 
dwellings.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Comments were made by the 
following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson and Mr. Segrave.   
 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending and updating the Solid Waste Fee Schedule 
in accordance with Section 8.44.010 of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
and repealing Ordinance No. 4503. Reporting for Finance Committee, Dr. Aldrich moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Cook voting “no”.  
    
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Sections 10.82.030 and 10.82.040 of the 
Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, modifying the term of Dockless Vehicle 
licenses, specifying an effective date and making other conforming amendments.  Dr. Aldrich 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.  Comments were made by the following members of the 
governing body: Mayor Collins and Mr. Johnson. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer rates, 
tap and system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and septic 
and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities – referred to 
Finance Committee.  (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne)      
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Chapter 5.78, Towing and Wrecker Recovery 
Operations, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, modifying provisions governing towing and wrecker recovery operation 
licensure and the Police Department’s towing and wrecker recovery operation rotation list – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)        
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming for the various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2024 and ending June 30, 2025 – referred 
to Committee of the Whole.  (Sponsor – Mr. Esquibel) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Adopting the City of Cheyenne 2024 Program Year Annual Action Plan 
prepared by the City of Cheyenne Housing & Community Development Office.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6401) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Approving water and/or sanitary sewer service, by Outside User 
Agreement, for Robert GrandPre, for the property at Lot 1, Block 1, Green Subdivision, 4th Filing, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (east of Ridge Road and north of and adjacent to Green Court).  Mr. 
Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. Public comment was made by: Bob GrandPre.  Comments were made by 
the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. 
Johnson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Segrave. (#6402) 

[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Adopting the fee schedule setting the approved towing service rates for 
towing charges related to calls for service by the Cheyenne Police Department made pursuant to 
the tow rotation list – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)     
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Expressing the support of the Governing Body for the submission of an 
application by the Cheyenne Transit Program to the Wyoming Department of Transportation for 
grant funding under the Wyoming Rural Public Transit Program – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Dr. Aldrich) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Supporting the submission of an application to the Wyoming Business 
Council under the Wyoming Business Ready Community Grant and Loan Program for an 
Enhancement Grant in support of the development of the Precision Industrial Park – referred to 
Finance Committee.  (Sponsor – Dr. Aldrich)     
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Cheyenne Business Parkway, Thirteenth Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming 
(located west of Christensen Road and south of and adjacent to Campstool Road) – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)     
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Centennial Heritage, 3rd Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located east of 
Savannah Drive and south and north of Raleigh Drive) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)     
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Approving sanitary sewer service, by Outside User Agreement, for 
Laramie County, for the property known as the Archer Complex (south of the I-80 and Archer 
Parkway Interchange) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)   
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Approving the budget of the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board for the 
for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2024 and ending June 30, 2025 – referred to Finance 
Committee.  (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne)  
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Approving the budget of the Cheyenne Downtown Development 
Authority for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2024 and ending June 30, 2025 – referred to 
Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
Improvement Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and DB Cheyenne, LLC for 
acceptance of cash-in-lieu for public improvements including a turn lane, as required by the UDC 
and Traffic Impact Study for the development. Dr. Aldrich moved to approve in an amount not to 
exceed $70,500.00, seconded by Mr. White. Dr. Aldrich moved to amend by substitute dated May 
6, 2024, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion to amend by substitute carried. Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”.  Comments were 
made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Cook and 
Mr. Esquibel. (#7964) 
  
 

 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Univerus, Inc., for the 
Sanitation Rerouting and Optimization Project (Solid Waste Fund).  Dr. Aldrich moved to approve 
in an amount not to exceed $142,676.00, seconded by Mr. White. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Comments 
were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, 
Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White. (#7965)  
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Landmark Aquatics, 
for the Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project (2021 Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund) – 
referred to Finance Committee.      
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and TPT Trails, LLC for 
the Belvoir Ranch, Phase 2 Design Build Trail Project (Belvoir Recreation Fund) – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #2 to Contract #7775 between the City of Cheyenne and Sunrise 
Engineering for additional engineering services for the 7th Street Drainage and Access 
Improvements Project (2019-2022 Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried (in an amount not to exceed $6,500.00). (#7775)    
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7897 between the City of Cheyenne and Richardson 
Construction, Inc. for the Gymnastics Facility Project (2019-2022 Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $74,292.50). (#7897) 
 
[CA]

[CA]

[CA]

[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-12-24 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and JTL 
Group Inc. d/b/a Knife River, for the 2024 City Overlay #1 Project (2021 Specific Purpose Option 
Tax Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted the bid in an amount not to exceed 
$2,192,669.75). (#7966) 
 
 
     
                       

[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-13-24 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Simon 
Contractors, for the 2024 City Overlay #2 Project (2021 Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund.  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted the bid in an amount not to exceed $1,518,379.60). 
(#7967) 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-15-24 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Asphalt Preservation, LLC, for the 2024 Surface Seal Project #2 (2021 Specific Purpose Option 
Tax Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted the bid in an amount not to exceed 
$2,084,128.00). (#7968) 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-10-24 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Reiman Corp for the HAWK Signals Construction Project (2019-2022 Optional 1% Sales Tax

[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-20-24 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Simon 
Contractors for the Public Works Improvements Project (2019-2022 Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund, 
Solid Waste Fund, & Fleet Maintenance Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-21-24 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Spire

Consideration of Bid #S-22-24 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Richardson Construction, Inc. for the Hitching Post Storm Sewer and Paul Smith Way Project

[CA] 
Request to amend a previously approved outdoor seating area for consumption by adding 
an additional area relating to City of Cheyenne Bar & Grill liquor license held by Paris West 
Restaurant and Bar, located at 1719 Carey Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
 
Urban Renewal Authority 2023 Annual Report. Mr. Esquibel moved to acknowledge 
receipt, seconded by Mr. Johnson. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.  Comment was made by the following member of the governing body: Mr. Johnson. 
 
 
No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
Announcement was made that due to the Memorial Day holiday on Monday, May 27, 2024, 
the regularly scheduled City Council meeting will instead be held on Tuesday, May 28, 2024.  The 
meeting will be held at 2101 O’Neil Avenue in City Council Chambers and via electronic 
conference meeting at 6:00 p.m.  
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held May 28, 2024 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting, 
to hear public comment regarding the City’s intent to apply for a 2024 Wyoming Business Council 
Business Ready Community Grant and Loan Program for a Community Readiness Project for 
essential infrastructure at the Precision Industrial Park. 

Public comment was made by the following under Other Business: Ed Galavotti. 
 
The following members of the governing body made comments under other business: Dr. 
Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson and Mr. White. 
 
Mr. Esquibel moved to meet in executive session Pursuant to Wyoming Statutes §16-4- 
405(a)(iii), seconded by Mr. Roybal. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.  Mayor Collins announced members would meet in executive session, not open to the public 
and stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result of the executive 
session, and that the regular City Council meeting would officially conclude at the close of the 
executive session. The following staff members were in attendance: Amber Ash, Mayor’s Chief 
of Staff; Stefanie Boster, City Attorney; John Brodie, Deputy City Attorney; Charles Bloom, 
Planning & Development Director; Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development and Kris Jones, City 
Clerk. [Clerk’s note: Mr. Segrave was absent for Executive Session] 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:06 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 Kristina F. Jones, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
May 22, 2024

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-05-08_TotalExtraction_64436355.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Connor White, Planner II 
Subject: PLTA-24-2 – Amending UDC Sections 1.4.3 and 5.8.8  
Date: 
April 22, 2024 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a UDC Text Amendment. This is approved by Ordinance.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows:

The Planning Commission heard this item at their April 15, 2024, meeting and unanimously voted to

Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment

criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2.  That on April 15, 2024, the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Section 1.4.3 Defined Terms, of Article 1, General Provisions, of the 
Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in 
this section: 
 
Building, Detached. A building having no common wall connection  or structural 
attachment with another building. 
 
Section 4.  That Section 5.8.8 Accessory Buildings, of Article 5, Zoning Regulations, of 
the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out 
in this section: 
 
5.8.8 Accessory Buildings 
 
j. To be considered a separate building, the structures must not share any structural 
attachment and no part of either building may be within 5' of the other when 
viewed from plan view. Two structures closer than 5' or with a structural 
attachment shall be considered one building for the purposes of setback, area, and 
number. 
 
Section 5.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 6.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

PLTA-24-2 
 
 
April 15, 2024 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
APRIL 15, 2024 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code Section 1.4.3 and 
5.8.8 
 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-24-2 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Sections 1.4.3 and 5.8.8 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests 
meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The proposed text amendments are to make it clearer as to the definition of a detached building and how 
close an accessory building can be to other buildings on a residential property before they are considered 
attached. The reason for this text amendment is due to several issues that have arisen with structures 
being built adjacent to the principal building and then later being attached.  
 
Currently the definition for a detached building states that there is no common wall connection with 
another building. There is nothing in the UDC that states how far an accessory building must be from 
another building or the principal building on a residential property. This means that you can place an 
accessory building within inches of another building or even the home. Due to this, there have been 
buildings built and then later attached to the principal building which creates many issues with setback 
requirements and even building code and fire code requirements.  
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-24-2 
 
 
April 15, 2024 
Staff proposes changing the definition of a detached building slightly. The change would add that a 
detached building also does not have any structural attachments to another building. The full definition 
will state that there is no common wall or structural attachment, which will make the definition even 
clearer for what a detached building is.  
 
Staff is also proposing adding a requirement for accessory buildings to be at least 1’ from other buildings 
on the property to be considered detached or a separate building. This requirement makes a clear 
requirement for how far a building needs to be from another to be considered detached or separate. This 
will allow staff to determine setbacks easier and it will reduce the number of accessory buildings that 
become attached to the principal building. 
 
Attached to this staff report are examples of some of the issues staff have run into. This is not to call out 
these property owners or point blame, but to show why staff has brought forward this amendment to 
make things clearer within the code.  
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
Amend UDC Section 1.4.3, Defined Terms, specifically Building, Detached. 
 
Building, Detached. A building having no common wall connection or structural attachment with another 
building. 
 
Additionally, amend UDC Section 5.8.8, Accessory Buildings to add the following: 
 
j. To be considered a separate building, the structures must not share any structural attachment and no 
part of either building may be within 1' of the other when viewed from plan view. Two structures closer 
than 1' or with a structural attachment shall be considered one building for the purposes of setback, area, 
and number. 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
This proposed text amendment is necessary to address an issue not anticipated in the 
Comprehensive Plan when the Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted. This issue was also 
not addressed when staff did a previous text amendment for accessory buildings. This 
amendment does not change the use of the property but provides clearer standards for what a 
detached / accessory building is.  
    
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
 

PLTA-24-2 
 
 
April 15, 2024 
(i)  Regulate and restrict the: 
(A)  Height, number of stories and size of buildings and 
other structures; 
(B)  Percentage of lot that may be occupied; 
(C)  Size of yards, courts and other open spaces; 
(D)  Density of population; and 
(E)  Location and use of buildings, structures and land for 
trade, industry, residence or other purposes. 
 (ii)  Establish setback building lines. 
 
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to clarify the 
definition for detached buildings and to make a clearer requirement for accessory buildings in 
relation to how close they can be to a principal building or another building.   
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment will make it clear as to what will be considered an accessory building. It will allow 
staff to better determine setbacks for accessory buildings and prevent potential fire and building 
code issues.  
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meet the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code Sections 1.4.3 and 
5.8.8 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the amendment meets the 
review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.

ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SUBSECTIONS 6.5.4.b.3 INCENTIVES / 
BONUS, 
6.5.4.b.4 
CREATIVE 
SIGN 
PROGRAM, 
AND 
6.5.8.j 
BILLBOARDS OF THE 
UNIFIED 
DEVELOPMENT 
CODE, 
TO 
RESTRUCTURE SIGN INCENTIVES AND BONUSES BY MODIFYING 
ALLOWANCES 
FOR 
SIGNS 
NEAR 
INTERSTATE 
HIGHWAYS, 
ALLOWANCES FOR SHARED SIGNS, ELIMINATING THE CREATIVE 
SIGN PROGRAM, AND CLARIFYING APPLICABILITY OF CERTAIN 
BILLBOARD ALLOWANCES." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
to the Unified Development Code (UDC) that is the subject of this ordinance meets the review 
criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2.  That on April 15, 2024, the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Subsection 6.5.4.b.3 Incentives / Bonus, of Subsection 6.5.4.b Mixed-Use, 
Commercial and Industrial Zoning Districts, of Subsection 6.5.4 Sign Allowances, of Section 6.5 
Signs, of Article 6 Design Regulations, of the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in this section: 
 
3. Incentives / Bonuses. 
(a) Materials. A 10% bonus in the maximum permitted sign allowance for Freestanding 
Signs may be provided if a Freestanding Sign is designed to integrate with the building 
structure. The sign is considered integrated if the same or similar building materials 
and colors are used. If there is disagreement whether a sign is integrated, the Director 
shall make a final decision. The bonus can be applied to both height and area. 
(b) Landscape. A 10% bonus in the maximum permitted sign allowance for Freestanding 
Signs may be provided if a Freestanding Sign is located within a landscaped area. The 
bonus may be granted if a minimum of 4 square feet of landscaping is provided for 
every 1 square foot of sign area. If the Freestanding Sign is integrated into a raised 
planter box, the landscaped area may be reduced to one square foot of landscaping for 
every one square foot of sign area. The bonus can be applied to both height and area. 
(c) Building Location. A 10% bonus in the maximum permitted sign allowance for 
Building Signs and Pedestrian Signs may be provided in the MUR, MUB, NB, and CB 
districts where the building is located within 10’ of the street right-of-way, and where 
no Freestanding Ground Signs are constructed. 
(d) Master Sign. Any sign approved as a Master Sign through the Master Sign Program (as 
outlined in Section 6.5.5.e) may have an increase in area and height as follows: 
i. Area. To qualify for an area bonus, a minimum of 4 tenants must contribute to the 
Master Sign. The area on a Master Sign may be increased by 50% for every sign 
12:42 pm, Apr 16 2024

Page 2 of 3 
 
removed or by 8 square feet per tenant space on the sign – whichever is greater. 
However, in no case shall the sign exceed 400 square feet in total area. 
ii. Height. To qualify for a height bonus, a minimum of 4 tenants must contribute to 
the Master Sign. The maximum height of a qualifying Low-Profile Master Sign is 
75% of the maximum height of a Freestanding Sign placed at the same location as 
the Master Sign. The maximum height of a qualifying Freestanding Master Sign is 
125% of the Freestanding Sign’s height allowance. 
(e) Near-interstate signs. Any parcel in a non-residential zone district and within 1000 ft of 
the I-25 or I-80 rights of way may have one extra Freestanding Sign which may have a 
bonus in area and height with the following regulations: 
i. 30’ minimum clearance between the ground and the base of the sign 
ii. 60’ maximum height 
iii. 200 square feet maximum area  
iv. be located on the half of the parcel closest to the interstate right-of-way 
v. EMC size maximum may be increased from 25% of the allowed sign area to 40% 
of the allowed sign area 
vi. If the parcel abuts the interstate right-of-way, this ‘extra’ sign replaces the 
allowance for a ground sign for the interstate right-of-way street frontage 
 
Section 4.  That Subsection 6.5.4.b.4 Creative Sign Program, of Subsection 6.5.4.b Mixed-
Use, Commercial and Industrial Zoning Districts, of Subsection 6.5.4 Sign Allowances, of Section 
6.5 Signs, of Article 6 Design Regulations, of the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, is eliminated in its entirety. 
 
Section 5.  That Subsection 6.5.8.j, of Subsection 6.5.8 Billboards, of Subsection 6.5.4, of 
Section 6.5 Signs, of Article 6 Design Regulations, of the Unified Development Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in this section: 
 
j. Billboards and on-premises signs on properties within 1,000’ of Interstate 25 and Interstate 
80 right-of-way shall conform to the following: 
1. Billboards and on-premises signs shall not exceed 672 square feet in area. Proof of 
compliance with applicable laws, rules and regulations of the State of Wyoming shall 
be presented to the building official along with sign permit applications. 
2. Billboards and on-premises signs on properties adjacent to within 1000’ of interstates 
on the same side of the interstate shall be at least 500’ apart. 
3. Billboards and on-premises signs shall not exceed 60’ in height. 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

PLTA-24-3  
 
 
April 15, 2024 
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
APRIL 15, 2024 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending subsections 6.5.4.b.3 and 6.5.8.j of the Unified 
Development Code (UDC), changing sign incentives to allow larger signs for 
interstates and for master signs and to clarify associated language 
    
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-24-3 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
subsections 6.5.4.b.3 and 6.5.8.j as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the 
requests meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The proposed text amendment is to change sections of the sign code to clarify incentives and bonuses. 
With the adoption of the UDC, signs were not allowed to go over 20 feet in height, and signs 20 feet high 
were limited to 70 square feet of area. This is not conducive to communicating to traffic an interstate. The 
City code has a Master Sign Program encouraging consolidating signs, but does not give any bonuses to 
the consolidated sign. To resolve this issue, applicants have had to go through assorted processes to get 
their signs approved – most frequently via the variance process. However, the variance process is 
supposed to be for properties in a unique situation that is not applicable to other properties in the area. 
 
Staff met with the Council in January 2024 in a work session to discuss various UDC issues and to get 
feedback on what to prioritize. Although a comprehensive sign amendment may be warranted, it 
appeared to Staff that the Council was lukewarm on the idea. When Staff mentioned the need to update 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-24-3  
 
 
April 15, 2024 
 
specific sections of sign code which have been problematic (especially highway signage), it appeared to 
Staff that the Council was more amenable to hearing this type of item. 
 
Generally, the proposed amendments has four parts.  
 
The first part creates a new bonus for highway signs. This is for properties is within 1,000 feet of I-80 and  
I-25, requires a minimum clearance (to encourage communication with the interstate), and allows for a 
larger height and area allowance. 
 
The second is the creation of a bonus for signs which have received approval as Master Sign Program 
pursuant to UDC 6.5.5.c.  This allows for an increase in height and area as an incentive to co-locate, and 
for effective communication when multiple tenants are in a complex. 
 
The third part is the removal of the Creative Sign Program. This part of the UDC has problems with not 
including objective review criteria and can lead to arbitrary decisions or could result in the Program 
becoming a path around the sign code.  
 
The fourth item is clarifying language found in Section 6.5.8.j of the Unified Development Code. Section 
6.5.8 is generally for Billboards, but contains the text “on premises signs” in 6.5.8.j. This has led to some 
confusion on code interpretation by Staff and applicants.  
 
This is likely an unintended copy-paste error when language from Title 17 was brought into the UDC, and 
Staff has interpreted this section as a copy-paste error. To take the section at face value, sites near the 
interstate would only be allowed one sign on their entire site, including building, incidental, window, and 
ground signs – as it would put a restriction of a 500’ buffer between ‘on premises’ signs. Although this 
section is not written as a ‘bonus’ for on-premises signs (superseding Section 6.5.4), if it were read or 
interpreted that way, billboard-sized signs could be placed on residential properties (as there is no zone 
directly mentioned in 6.5.8.j; and Section 6.5.8.a controlling what zones allow billboards does not include 
the “on premises signs” text).

STAFF COMMENTARY: 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
The Comprehensive plan encourages signage that identifies businesses without dominating the 
setting or skyline. However, the plan also calls for limited signage in community gateways (see 
“COMMUNITY GATEWAY” and Policy 5.6.A). The comprehensive plan also encourages the 
jurisdictions to manage or consolidate signage along major roadways and in commercial areas to 
and provide strong graphic character through 
the imaginative use of graphics, color, texture, 
quality materials, scale, and proportion.  
(b) Contextual Criteria. The sign shall contain at 
least one of the following elements: 
i. Creative image reflecting current or 
historic character of the City; 
ii. Symbols or imagery relating to the use of 
the property; 
iii. Inventive representation of the use, 
name, logo, structure or business. 
(c) Architectural Criteria. The sign utilizes and/or 
enhances the architectural elements of the 
building; and placed in a logical location in 
relation to the overall composition of the 
building’s façade surrounding uses and not 
cover any key architectural features/details of 
the façade. 
 
UDC 6.5.8.j 
j. Billboards and on-premises signs on properties within 
1,000’ of Interstate 25 and Interstate 80 right-of-way 
shall conform to the following: 
1. Billboards and on-premises signs shall not exceed 
672 square feet in area. Proof of compliance with 
applicable laws, rules and regulations of the State 
of Wyoming shall be presented to the building 
official along with sign permit applications. 
2. Billboards and on-premises signs on properties 
within 1,000’ of adjacent to interstates on the 
same side of the interstate shall be at least 500’ 
apart. 
3. Billboards and on-premises signs shall not exceed 
60’ in height. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Removes the questionable reference 
to ‘on premises signs’. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Removes ambiguity created by the 
term ‘adjacent’. 
 

PLTA-24-3  
 
 
April 15, 2024 
 
preserve scenic views, make identification of sites less confusing, and encourage more attractive 
sites. Unified signage design is also encouraged. New signage is proposed as a way to demonstrate 
re-investment in an existing commercial center.  
 
The Master Sign Program encourages the consolidation of signs, but gives no bonus to such 
consolidation. By providing a bonus to the Master Sign program, this program may be more 
frequently used to remove sign clutter and unify sign design. 
 
The main purpose of a sign is to promote communication with drivers to identify a desired 
destination. Signs that are too small or too short may defeat this purpose, but this purpose must 
be balanced with providing an attractive community. The current sign code does not allow ground 
signs which provide effective communication with the highway, but Staff’s proposal is a modest 
allowance to allow for communication without dominating the skyline. Due to the City’s stringent 
signage requirements, most entities re-use existing signs instead of proposing new signage. This 
makes the creation of new signage to demonstrate re-investment difficult.  
    
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
Regulations; scope and purpose; uniformity within authorized districts; 
to follow plan; objectives. 
  
(a)  The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
(i)  Regulate and restrict the: 
  
 
(A)  Height, number of stories and size of buildings and other structures; 
(B)  Percentage of lot that may be occupied; 
  
 
(C)  Size of yards, courts and other open spaces; 
(D)  Density of population; and 
  
 
(E) Location and use of buildings, structures and land for trade, industry, 
residence or other purposes. 
  
(ii)  Establish setback building lines. 
  
(b)  The governing body may divide the city or town into districts of such number, shape and area 
as it deems necessary, and within those districts it may regulate and restrict the erection, 
construction, reconstruction, alteration, repair or use of buildings, structures or land. 
  
(c)  Regulations may differ from one (1) district to another but shall be uniform for each class or 
kind of buildings within a district. 
  
(d)  All regulations shall be made: 
(i)  In accordance with a comprehensive plan and designed to: 
  
 
(A)  Lessen congestion in the streets; 
  
 
(B)  Secure safety from fire, panic and other dangers; 
  
 
(C)  Promote health and general welfare; 
  
 
(D)  Provide adequate light and air; 
  
 
(E)  Prevent the overcrowding of land; 
  
 
(F)  Avoid undue concentration of population; and 
  
 
(G)  Facilitate adequate provisions for transportation, water, sewerage, schools, 
parks and other public requirements. 

PLTA-24-3  
 
 
April 15, 2024 
 
(ii)  With reasonable consideration, among other things, of the character of the district 
and its peculiar suitability for particular uses; 
  
(iii)  With a view to conserving the value of buildings and encouraging the most 
appropriate use of land throughout the city or town; and 
(iv)  With consideration given to the historic integrity of certain neighborhoods or districts 
and a view to preserving, rehabilitating and maintaining historic properties and encouraging 
compatible uses within the neighborhoods or districts, but no regulation made to carry out the 
purposes of this paragraph is valid to the extent it constitutes an unconstitutional taking without 
compensation. 
 
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to modify 
sections of code to regulate sign height and area (structural components). It also proposes a 
balance between effective communication and keeping the skyline from being too crowded. 
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment would not adversely impact the general health, safety and public welfare of 
Cheyenne. Increasing the height and area of certain signs allows signs to preform their function 
of communicating location of a business. Better communication reduces the potential for 
swerving vehicles or having eyes removed from the road for a prolonged period of time tying to 
find and read signage. Putting limits on those increases reduces the potential for sign clutter or 
excessively large signs.  
 
 
OUTREACH:  
 
Staff sent an email to all contractors who were on a sign permit submitted since January 1, 2024. No 
substantive comments on the proposal were received at the time of writing this Staff report.  
This text amendment is based on Staff’s interactions with various applicants requesting signage from the 
adoption of the UDC into this year (2024). 
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code subsections 6.5.4.b.3 
and 6.5.8.j as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests meets the review 
criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.

Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
to the Unified Development Code (UDC) that is the subject of this ordinance meets the review 
criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC.

3. Incentives / Bonuses. 
(a) Materials. A 10% bonus in the maximum permitted sign allowance for Freestanding 
Signs may be provided if a Freestanding Sign is designed to integrate with the building 
structure. The sign is considered integrated if the same or similar building materials 
and colors are used. If there is disagreement whether a sign is integrated, the Director 
shall make a final decision. The bonus can be applied to both height and area. 
(b) Landscape. A 10% bonus in the maximum permitted sign allowance for Freestanding 
Signs may be provided if a Freestanding Sign is located within a landscaped area. The

every 1 square foot of sign area. If the Freestanding Sign is integrated into a raised 
planter box, the landscaped area may be reduced to one square foot of landscaping for 
every one square foot of sign area. The bonus can be applied to both height and area.

Building Signs and Pedestrian Signs may be provided in the MUR, MUB, NB, and CB 
districts where the building is located within 10’ of the street right-of-way, and where 
no Freestanding Ground Signs are constructed. 
(d) Master Sign. Any sign approved as a Master Sign through the Master Sign Program (as 
outlined in Section 6.5.5.e) may have an increase in area and height as follows: 
i. Area. To qualify for an area bonus, a minimum of 4 tenants must contribute to the 
Master Sign. The area on a Master Sign may be increased by 50% for every sign

Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the Unified 
Development Code. 
 
Section 2.  That on April 15, 2024, the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Section 5.1.5, Residential Buildings and Lot Types, of Article 5, Zoning 
Regulations, General Provisions, of the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, is hereby amended to allow up to three (3) stories for Lot Types DD1, DD2, DD3, DD4, 
DD5, DD6, DD7, DD8, SD1, SD2, SD3, AD1, AD2, and AD3. 
 
Section 4.  That Section 5.1.5, Residential Buildings and Lot Types, of Article 5, Zoning 
Regulations, General Provisions, of the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, is hereby amended to change Accessory Building Height to "In no case higher than the 
principal building" for Lot Types DD3, DD4, DD5, DD6, DD7, DD8, SD1, SD2, SD3, AD1, AD2, 
and AD3. 
 
Section 5.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 6.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
PLANNING COMMISSION 
APRIL 15, 2024 
STAFF REPORT  
REQUEST: 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code Section 5.1.5. 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-24-1 
PREPARED BY: 
Sophia Maes, Planner I, Planning and Development Department 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Section 5.1.5 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests meet the 
review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
BACKGROUND: 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
The proposed text amendment is one of several amendments based on the Affordable Housing Task Force 
recommendations. One of these recommendations is to increase habitable space by allowing up to three 
(3) stories for residential properties (principal and accessory buildings). Staff reviewed the many Lot Types
that are within the UDC and found the allowance of up to three (3) stories would be appropriate for the
following lot types: Lot Types DD1, DD2, DD3, DD4, DD5, DD6, DD7, DD8, SD1, SD2, SD3, AD1, AD2, and
AD3. See the attached exhibit for the proposed amendments.
The current UDC allows up to 2.5 stories for principal buildings and up to 2 stories for accessory buildings 
for the low-density residential lot types. The benefits of increasing the maximum height for principal and 
accessory buildings in these lot types outweigh the perceived costs. The perceived disadvantage of this 
amendment is solely the effects of increased heights in residential buildings. The increased height may 
not be favorable to neighbors, particularly if they worry this additional height will disrupt their view or 
Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
1

crowd the airspace. However, three-story buildings are possible to build as low-rise, adding to density and 
land efficiency without increased view disruptions. The 2021 IRC states residential “wood framed 
buildings” are allowed up to “three stories above grade plane in height” for health and safety.1 While a 
2.5-story building fits within this code, the IRC requirements for half-stories are misaligned with the UDC, 
rendering almost 100% of half-stories in Cheyenne unhabitable space. For a half-story with a sloped roof 
to contain habitable space per the 2021 IRC, at least 50% of the space must have a 7-foot ceiling, and no 
portion can have less than a 5-foot ceiling.2 This renders many half stories (per the UDC definitions) 100% 
unhabitable.3 For example, an 8/12 roof pitch 2.5-story building (9’ per full story,4 1368 SF Floor Area 
each) would have a difference of less than 7’ for an 8/15 roof pitch 3-story building, while adding almost 
1368 SF of habitable space (see Exhibit B). A 3-story residential building at around 39 feet in height will 
likely not constrict the view any more than a 2.5 story building at 32.5 feet, yet the additional habitable 
square footage increases from 0 square feet to 1,368 square feet, resulting in 4,104 Total SF (vs 2,736 
Total SF for the 2.5-story). Not only is this important for lots holding duplexes and townhomes, but also 
for single family homes where the additional square footage without increasing lot size will help 
affordability and increase quality of daily life for young and growing families. 
Additionally, this increase in maximum stories for residential lot types will allow maximum utilization of 
smaller infill lots that may otherwise not be worth the cost of developing (per developer calculations). 
This is relevant to the SD (Duplex/Semi-attached) and AD (Townhome/Attached) lot type. It is important 
to note that this text amendment does not require 3 stories, only that it allows up to three stories. This 
will increase land efficiency without lateral expansion, while still conforming to current UDC setback 
requirements. According to “Breaking the Code,” (a toolkit for Wyoming housing goals), a townhome 
development (AD1-3) “can easily achieve densities of 25 units per acre.”5 This will allow property owners 
and developers more flexibility, creating a diverse housing mix and increasing infill opportunities to meet 
the needs of a growing Cheyenne and adhering to PlanCheyenne Policy 2.3.A Mix of Housing Types 
(PlanCheyenne, page 60).  
Staff believes that the proposed text amendment will allow for higher density in infill and new 
neighborhoods, increasing residential units and housing type mixes. This will be beneficial for the 
community, providing additional housing options and maximizing land and infrastructure resources.  
1 2021 IRC §301.2.2.7 
2 2021 IRC §R305.1 
3 UDC §1.4.2.k.2: Half-story. A space under a sloping roof that has the line of intersection of the roof and the exterior wall 
supporting the roof not more than 3’ above the floor level, and in which space the possible floor area with head room of 6’ or 
more occupies between 30% and 70% of the total floor area of the story directly beneath. 
4 UDC §1.4.2.k.1: Story. The portion of a building included between the surface of any floor and the surface of the floor next 
above it, or if there is no floor above it, then the space between the floor and the ceiling next above it or, where there is not a 
ceiling, to the top of the roof rafters. 
a.
Stories shall begin at the first-floor level that is between 3’ above finished grade and 4’ below grade.
b.
The minimum story shall be 9’ and the maximum story shall be 14’, except that the height of the first story may be as
high as 20’ for all non-residential buildings that are 2 stories or more.
5 Wyoming Business Council, et al.“Breaking the Code: Aligning Zoning Code with Housing Goals. A toolkit for Wyoming 
communities.” pg. 30. 
2

PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
The proposed amendments are to allow up to three (3) stories for residential properties (principal and 
accessory buildings) for Lot Types DD1, DD2, DD3, DD4, DD5, DD6, DD7, DD8, SD1, SD2, SD3, AD1, AD2, 
and AD3. See the attached exhibit for the proposed amendments. 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
1.
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not
inconsistent with the plan.”
The proposed amendment is in compliance with multiple Comprehensive Plan goals and policies.
•
Policy 1.1.B Flexibility to respond to market demands:
•
Policy 1.4.A Infill Opportunities:
•
Policy 1.4.D Infill and Revitalization Incentives
•
Policy 2.3.A Mix of Housing Types:
•
Policy 2.3.B: Higher Density Housing
•
Policy 2.5.A: Community Housing Needs Met
Allowing 3-story residential buildings (principal and accessory) allows developers to adjust developments 
based on housing market demands, utilization of infill properties for higher density and affordability, and 
flexibility of the housing type mix. No residential building must be 3-stories, yet the allowance positions 
developers and owners the flexibility to provide more habitable square footage per parcel. This directly 
affects the current and projected severe market demands of housing in Cheyenne, allowing more housing 
units and space to be provided with limited additional land costs. Cheyenne has many infill properties that 
could be attractive to developers if additional square footage could be built in one development. Allowing 
3-stories for residential buildings will allow maximum utilization of smaller infill lots that may otherwise
not be worth the cost of developing. As stated above, a dramatic increase in habitable space can be
provided without additional land and minimal infrastructure additions, resulting in a lower rents overall
required to reach developer target ROIs and higher density of housing (another tool of land and resource
efficiency). This flexibility in stories allowed creates opportunities for a mix of housing types, allowing
variety of spaces to fit the needs of various resident types.
2.
STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute.
WYO 15-1-601. 
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may:
(i) Regulate and restrict the:
(A) Height, number of stories and size of buildings and
other structures;
(B) Percentage of lot that may be occupied;
(C) Size of yards, courts and other open spaces;
3

(D) Density of population; and
(E) Location and use of buildings, structures and land for
trade, industry, residence or other purposes.
(ii) Establish setback building lines.
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to allow up to 
three (3) stories for residential lot types DD1-AD3. This does not affect height restrictions, 
setbacks, lot coverage, or allowed uses per established zone districts.   
3.
HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.”
This amendment would allow residential buildings (principal and accessory) up to 3 stories,
increasing habitable square footage per lot and simplifying the review and inspection process. The 
2021 IRC allows all residential building types up to 3 stories. The City of Cheyenne Building
Department has reviewed the proposed amendment, stating it complies with one-family, two-
family, and townhome scope and provisions of the current adopted International Residential Code 
(IRC) of "not more than three stories above grade plane in height" (IRC §301.2.2.7). Fire Safety
has also reviewed this proposal and has no concerns. This amendment would align with the IRC
code our Building Inspectors utilize, as well as many residential building standards throughout the
nation, and does not affect the general health, safety, and public welfare.
CONCLUSION:  
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code Section 5.1.5 as 
outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests meet the review criteria of 
UDC 2.4.1.d.

2023 Housing Study

Exhibit B — Square footage difference (2.5 story vs 3 story)

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING AND UPDATING THE SOLID WASTE 
FEE SCHEDULE IN ACCORDANCE WITH SECTION 8.44.010 OF THE 
MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, AND 
REPEALING ORDINANCE NO. 4503."  
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Cheyenne City Ordinance No. 4503, adopted by the governing body on 
May 8, 2023, authorized the fee schedule currently applicable to solid waste services under Title 8, 
Chapter 8.44, of the Cheyenne City Code. 
 
Section 2.  That, effective July 1, 2024, the fee schedule for solid waste services is hereby 
repealed, replaced and otherwise superseded by the schedule appended to this ordinance, which is 
incorporated herein by reference. 
 
Section 3.  That Cheyenne City Ordinance No. 4503 is repealed effective July 1, 2024. 
 
 
Section 4.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.
