ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTIONS 1.4.3 DEFINED TERMS AND 
2.2.6 TEMPORARY USE APPROVAL OF THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT 
CODE, ADDING A MINIMUM OF FOURTEEN DAYS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
to the Unified Development Code (UDC) that is the subject of this ordinance meets the review 
criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2.  That on May 20, 2024, the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Section 1.4.3 Defined Terms, of Article 1, General Provisions, of the 
Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in 
this section: 
 
 
Temporary Use. A use or activity that is allowed in the district and which is intended to 
occupy the site on a non-continuous basis for more than 14 days. 
 
Section 4.  That Section 2.2.6 Temporary Use Approval, of Article 2, Applications, 
Procedures and Criteria, of the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is 
hereby amended as set out in this section: 
 
2.2.6 Temporary Use Approval 
 
a. Intent. A temporary use approval is intended to provide flexibility for different uses 
within a zoning district, and specifically allow the potential for uses that are not 
generally appropriate in a zoning district, but due to the short duration, special 
conditions relative to the specific site, and other case-specific issues may be 
acceptable for a short and limited time. 
b. Applicability. An application for a temporary use may be initiated by the property 
owner or authorized agent for any use within an existing zoning district that 
occupies the site on a non-continuous basis for more than 14 days.is not otherwise 
allowed. 
 
Section 5.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 6.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

PLTA-24-4 
 
 
May 20, 2024 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
MAY 20, 2024 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code Section 1.4.3 and 
2.2.6. 
 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-24-4 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Sections 1.4.3 and 2.2.6 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests 
meet the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The proposed text amendments are to make clarifications to temporary uses. The amendment adds  that 
a use will be required to apply for temporary use approval if it occupies a location on a non-continuous 
basis for more than 14 days. Anything that is less than 14 days and not permanent will not require a 
temporary use application or approval.  
 
This text amendment is mainly to address temporary uses that happen around Cheyenne Frontier Days 
(CFD). Every year there are multiple temporary uses that tend to show up for just the week of CFD. We 
would then require them to apply for a temporary use application which has a review period of at least 7 
days. At the same time, many of the uses also need to get licensed with the Clerks office. There have been 
times where the temporary use application come in late and then there is a rush to get everything 
processed in time for the Clerks office to also issue the license. CFD is roughly a 10 day event, this 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-24-4 
 
 
May 20, 2024 
amendment would make it so that a temporary use application for any use proposed to operate for the 
duration of CFD would not be required.  
 
Staff chose 14 days because a Zoning Violations first notice gives 14 days to remedy the violation. The 
uses that come for CFD are here for just its duration so if they receive a violation letter they will have it 
remedied by the end of CFD as they generally leave once it concludes.   
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
Amend UDC Section 1.4.3, Defined Terms, specifically Temporary Use. 
 
Temporary Use. A use or activity that is allowed in the district and which is intended to occupy the site 
on a non-continuous basis for more than 14 days. 
 
Additionally, amend UDC Section 2.2.6, Temporary Use Approval. 
 
a. Intent. A temporary use approval is intended to provide flexibility for different uses within a zoning 
district, and specifically allow the potential for uses that are not generally appropriate in a zoning district, 
but due to the short duration, special conditions relative to the specific site, and other case-specific issues 
may be acceptable for a short and limited time. 
 
b. Applicability. An application for a temporary use may be initiated by the property owner or 
authorized agent for any use within an existing zoning district that occupies the site on a non-continuous 
basis for more than 14 days is not otherwise allowed. 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
This proposed text amendment is necessary to address an issue not anticipated in the 
Comprehensive Plan when the Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted. Temporary uses 
are meant to be short in duration and not permanent. Anything less than two weeks would not 
be considered permanent and is very temporary in nature.  
    
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
 
(b) The governing body may divide the city or town into districts 
of such number, shape and area as it deems necessary, and 
within those districts it may regulate and restrict the erection, 
construction, reconstruction, alteration, repair or use of 
buildings, structures or land. 
 

PLTA-24-4 
 
 
May 20, 2024 
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to change the 
initial duration of a temporary use to eliminate the need for temporary uses that are less than 14 
days in length. This is allowed by state statutes as the City can dictate the length of time and use 
of land.  
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment provides additional clarification for temporary uses and lines up temporary uses 
with zoning violation first notices. This amendment will also allow uses who participate in CFD 
have an easier time getting through City processes in time for the event.   
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meet the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code Sections 1.4.3 and 
2.2.6 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the amendments meet the 
review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.
